=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Takahashi and Aoki will play a game using cards with numbers written on them.
Initially, Takahashi has N cards with numbers A_1, \ldots, A_N in his hand, Aoki has M cards with numbers B_1, \ldots, B_M in his hand, and there are L cards with numbers C_1, \ldots, C_L on the table.
Throughout the game, both Takahashi and Aoki know all the numbers on all the cards, including the opponent's hand.
Starting with Takahashi, they take turns performing the following action:

- Choose one card from his hand and put it on the table. Then, if there is a card on the table with a number less than the number on the card he just played, he may take one such card from the table into his hand.

The player who cannot make a move first loses, and the other player wins. Determine who wins if both players play optimally.
It can be proved that the game always ends in a finite number of moves.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M L
A_1 \ldots A_N
B_1 \ldots B_M
C_1 \ldots C_L

Output

Print Takahashi if Takahashi wins, and Aoki if Aoki wins.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N, M, L
- N + M + L \leq 12
- 1 \leq A_i, B_i, C_i \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

1 1 2
2
4
1 3

Sample Output 1

Aoki

The game may proceed as follows (not necessarily optimal moves):

- Takahashi plays 2 from his hand to the table, and takes 1 from the table into his hand. Now, Takahashi's hand is (1), Aoki's hand is (4), and the table cards are (2,3).
- Aoki plays 4 from his hand to the table, and takes 2 into his hand. Now, Takahashi's hand is (1), Aoki's hand is (2), and the table cards are (3,4).
- Takahashi plays 1 from his hand to the table. Now, Takahashi's hand is (), Aoki's hand is (2), and the table cards are (1,3,4).
- Aoki plays 2 from his hand to the table. Now, Takahashi's hand is (), Aoki's hand is (), and the table cards are (1,2,3,4).
- Takahashi cannot make a move and loses; Aoki wins.

Sample Input 2

4 4 4
98 98765 987654 987654321
987 9876 9876543 98765432
123 12345 1234567 123456789

Sample Output 2

Takahashi

Sample Input 3

1 1 8
10
10
1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8

Sample Output 3

Aoki

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

State for recursion: (taka_mask, aoki_mask, turn) where turn 0 for Takahashi, 1 for Aoki. table_mask = all_mask ^ taka_mask ^ aoki_mask. Since taka_mask and aoki_mask disjoint. Need ensure no overlap; recursion maintains.

Pseudo:

def win(taka, aoki, turn):
    hand = taka if turn==0 else aoki
    if hand == 0:
        return False
    table = all_mask ^ taka ^ aoki
    opp = aoki if turn==0 else taka
    # iterate bits in hand
    h = hand
    while h:
        lb = h & -h
        i = lb.bit_length()-1
        vi = values[i]
        # no capture
        if turn==0:
            nt = taka ^ lb
            na = aoki
        else:
            nt = taka
            na = aoki ^ lb
        if not win(nt, na, 1-turn):
            return True
        # capture: choose j in table with values[j] < vi
        t = table
        while t:
            lb2 = t & -t
            j = lb2.bit_length()-1
            if values[j] < vi:
                if turn==0:
                    nt = (taka ^ lb) | lb2  # remove i add j
                    na = aoki
                else:
                    nt = taka
                    na = (aoki ^ lb) | lb2
                if not win(nt, na, 1-turn):
                    return True
            t ^= lb2
        h ^= lb
    return False

Need be careful: In capture, table card j is in table mask before move. After move, table mask = (table ^ lb2) | lb. Since lb is hand card i. In state, table implied; taka/aoki masks update as above. Terminal: if hand == 0, current player cannot move and loses. Return False. Is it possible current player's hand empty but opponent hand empty too? Then current loses, correct.

Need ensure code reads input. Input format:
N M L
A_1 ... A_N

All on lines but can use sys.stdin.read().split().

Build values:
data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.read().split()))
N,M,L=data[0:3]
A=data[3:3+N]
B=data[3+N:3+N+M]
C=data[3+N+M:3+N+M+L]
values = A+B+C
K=N+M+L
all_mask=(1<<K)-1
taka_mask=0
for i in range(N): taka_mask |= 1<<i
aoki_mask=0
for i in range(N, N+M): aoki_mask |= 1<<i
# table mask initial = all_mask ^ taka_mask ^ aoki_mask (bits N+M..K-1)
less_mask = [0]*K
for i in range(K):
    mask=0
    for j in range(K):
        if values[j] < values[i]:
            mask |= 1<<j
    less_mask[i]=mask

Then define win.

But we can reduce memory by using manual memo dict with encoded int key. Let's design a single integer encoding to reduce overhead and speed.

Encode state as code = taka_mask | (aoki_mask << K) | (turn << (2*K)). Since taka_mask and aoki_mask each K bits. K<=12, code < 2^24 *2 = 33,554,432. Use dictionary memo: dict[int, bool]. lru_cache with function(taka,aoki,turn) internally hashes tuple of 3 ints; manual dict with int key hashes int faster and less memory. But lru_cache is convenient. We can write:

memo = {}
def win(taka, aoki, turn):
    key = taka | (aoki << K) | (turn << (2*K))
    if key in memo: return memo[key]
    ...
    memo[key] = res
    return res

This avoids lru_cache overhead and tuple keys. Need ensure K maybe 12, shift 2*K=24, turn shift 24. taka and aoki masks <1<<K. Good. If K=12, code < 1<<25. Fine. If K=0? no.

Now, move generation optimization.

Inside win:
hand = taka if turn == 0 else aoki
if hand == 0: return False
table = all_mask ^ taka ^ aoki
# We can iterate over bits of hand. For each bit i:
h = hand
while h:
    lb = h & -h
    i = lb.bit_length() - 1
    # no capture
    if turn == 0:
        nt = taka ^ lb
        na = aoki
    else:
        nt = taka
        na = aoki ^ lb
    if not win(nt, na, turn ^ 1):
        memo[key] = True; return True
    # capture
    cand = table & less_mask[i]
    while cand:
        lb2 = cand & -cand
        # j = lb2.bit_length() - 1 not needed except maybe no
        if turn == 0:
            nt = (taka ^ lb) | lb2
            na = aoki
        else:
            nt = taka
            na = (aoki ^ lb) | lb2
        if not win(nt, na, turn ^ 1):
            memo[key] = True; return True
        cand ^= lb2
    h ^= lb
memo[key] = False
return False

Need note: In capture, lb2 is a bit from table. We don't need j index. less_mask[i] includes bits of cards with value < values[i]. Since table & less_mask[i] gives valid capture cards. Good.

Implementation:

def win(taka, aoki, turn):
    key = taka | (aoki << K) | (turn << (2*K))
    if key in memo:
        return memo[key]
    hand = taka if turn == 0 else aoki
    if hand == 0:
        memo[key] = False
        return False
    table = all_mask ^ taka ^ aoki
    h = hand
    while h:
        lb = h & -h
        i = lb.bit_length() - 1
        # no capture
        if turn == 0:
            nt = taka ^ lb
            na = aoki
        else:
            nt = taka
            na = aoki ^ lb
        if not win(nt, na, turn ^ 1):
            memo[key] = True
            return True
        cand = table & less_mask[i]
        while cand:
            lb2 = cand & -cand
            if turn == 0:
                nt = (taka ^ lb) | lb2
                na = aoki
            else:
                nt = taka
                na = (aoki ^ lb) | lb2
            if not win(nt, na, turn ^ 1):
                memo[key] = True
                return True
            cand ^= lb2
        h ^= lb
    memo[key] = False
    return False

Potential bug: In no-capture, if turn==0, nt = taka ^ lb. Since lb in taka, remove. If turn==1, na = aoki ^ lb. Good.

Potential bug: In capture, if turn==0, nt = (taka ^ lb) | lb2. Since lb in taka, remove; lb2 from table, add. But what if lb2 is already in taka? It can't because table. Good. If turn==1, na = (aoki ^ lb) | lb2.

Potential bug: We compute `table` before loop. If we take a capture, table changes in successor but we don't update current table for subsequent moves. We shouldn't; each move is independent from original state. Good.

Potential bug: In capture loop, `cand = table & less_mask[i]`; table is original table. Good.

Potential bug: We use `h ^= lb` to remove processed bit. Good.

": "```python\nimport sys\n\nsys.setrecursionlimit(1000000)\n\ndef main():\n    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.read().split()))\n    N, M, L = data[0], data[1], data[2]\n    A = data[3:3+N]\n    B = data[3+N:3+N+M]\n    C = data[3+N+M:3+N+M+L]\n\n    values = A + B + C\n    K = N + M + L\n    all_mask = (1 << K) - 1\n\n    taka_mask = 0\n    for i in range(N):\n        taka_mask |= 1 << i\n\n    aoki_mask = 0\n    for i in range(N, N + M):\n        aoki_mask |= 1 << i\n\n    less_mask = [0] * K\n    for i in range(K):\n        mask = 0\n        for j in range(K):\n            if values[j] < values[i]:\n                mask |= 1 << j\n        less_mask[i] = mask\n\n    memo = {}\n\n    def win(taka, aoki, turn):\n        # turn: 0 = Takahashi, 1 = Aoki\n        key = taka | (aoki << K) | (turn << (2 * K))\n        if key in memo:\n            return memo[key]\n\n        hand = taka if turn == 0 else aoki\n        if hand == 0:\n            memo[key] = False\n            return False\n\n        table = all_mask & ~(taka | aoki)\n        h = hand\n\n        while h:\n            lb = h & -h\n            i = lb.bit_length() - 1\n\n            # Do not take a card from the table.\n            if turn == 0:\n                nt = taka ^ lb\n                na = aoki\n            else:\n                nt = taka\n                na = aoki ^ lb\n\n            if not win(nt, na, turn ^ 1):\n                memo[key] = True\n                return True\n\n            # Take one table card whose number is smaller.\n            cand = table & less_mask[i]\n            while cand:\n                lb2 = cand & -cand\n\n                if turn == 0:\n                    nt = (taka ^ lb) | lb2\n                    na = aoki\n                else:\n                    nt = taka\n                    na = (aoki ^ lb) | lb2\n\n                if not win(nt, na, turn ^ 1):\n                    memo[key] = True\n                    return True\n\n                cand ^= lb2\n\n            h ^= lb\n\n        memo[key] = False\n        return False\n\n    if win(taka_mask, aoki_mask, 0):\n        print(\"Takahashi\")\n    else:\n        print(\"Aoki\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n```\n"
}
